y\neq z\end{subarray}}\frac{|f(y)-f(z)|}{d(y,z)}\quad\forall\ a\in\alpha,\ \forall\ x\in a.

For instance,
|f|_{\mathcal{B}}=\left\|D_{\alpha^{*}}f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(A,\mu)}
, and
D_{\alpha^{*}}f\geq D_{\alpha}f
since
\alpha^{*}
is coarser than
\alpha
.

Proposition A.5
.

Let
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
be a Gibbs-Markov map. There exists a constant
C
such that, for any stopping time
\varphi
and any
n\geq 1
, for any
\underline{a}\in\sigma(\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)})
, for any
f:A\to\mathbb{C}
,

\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}^{n}(f\mathbf{1}_{\underline{a}})\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-\inf_{\underline{a}}\varphi^{(n)}}\int_{\underline{a}}D_{\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}}f\;{\rm d}\mu+\int_{\underline{a}}|f|\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

(A.2)

Proof.

Let us assume that
\underline{a}=[a_{0},\ldots,a_{k-1}]
is a cylinder of length
k=\varphi^{(n)}(\underline{a})
. Then
\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}^{n}(f\mathbf{1}_{\underline{a}})=\mathcal{L}^{k}(f\mathbf{1}_{\underline{a}})
. In addition,
\Lambda^{-k}\leq\Lambda^{-\inf_{\underline{a}}\varphi^{(n)}}
. With these observations in mind, the claim is given by
[
21
, Lemme 1.1.13]
, with the refinement that the local Lipschitz seminorm can be taken on
\underline{a}
instead of
a_{0}
.

In general,
\underline{a}
is a countable union of such cylinders, and the claim follows by countable additivity of
\mu
.
∎

Proposition
A.5
yields a uniform Lasota-Yorke inequality for
\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}
for all stopping times
\varphi
:

Corollary A.6
.

Let
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
be a Gibbs-Markov map. There exists a constant
C
such that, for any stopping time
\varphi
and any
n\geq 1
, for any
f\in\mathcal{B}
,

\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}^{n}(f)\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}+\left\|f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{1}(A,\mu)}\right].

Proof.

We apply Proposition
A.5
with
\underline{a}=A
. The partition
\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}
is finer than
\alpha
, which is itself finer than
\alpha^{*}
, whence
D_{\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}}f\leq D_{\alpha^{*}}f
and

\int_{A}D_{\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}}f\;{\rm d}\mu\leq\int_{A}D_{\alpha^{*}}f\;{\rm d}\mu\leq\left\|D_{\alpha^{*}}f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(A,\mu)}=|f|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}.

Finally,
\inf_{A}\varphi\geq 1
so
\inf_{A}\varphi^{(n)}\geq n
.
∎

In particular,
\rho_{\ess}(\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B})\leq\Lambda^{-1}
, and the usual arguments imply the existence of finitely many absolutely continuous invariant probability measures for
T_{\varphi}
. Another application of Proposition
A.5
yields

Corollary A.7
.

Let
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
be a Gibbs-Markov map. There exists a constant
C
such that, for any stopping time
\varphi
and any
n\geq 1
, for any
\underline{a}\in\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}
and
f\in\mathcal{B}
,

\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}^{n}(f\mathbf{1}_{\underline{a}})\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq C\mu(\underline{a})\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}.

(A.3)

Proof.

We apply Proposition
A.5
. The same argument as in the proof of Corollary
A.6
yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi}^{n}(f\mathbf{1}_{\underline{a}})\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\int_{\underline{a}}D_{\alpha_{\varphi}^{(n)}}f\;{\rm d}\mu+\int_{\underline{a}}|f|\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\mu(\underline{a})\left\|D_{\alpha^{*}}f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(A,\mu)}+\mu(\underline{a})\left\|f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{\infty}(A,\mu)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\mu(\underline{a})\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}},

with the same constant
C
.
∎

A.3
Induced Gibbs-Markov maps

Let
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
be a Gibbs-Markov map, and
F:A\to\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be constant on the elements of
\alpha
. Let
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
be the associated
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-extension, which we assume is recurrent. Let
\Sigma\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be non-empty and finite. We want to find a good Banach space on which the transfer operator associated with the induced system
([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]},T_{[\Sigma]})
acts. A natural candidate is the space
\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}
defined by Equation (
1.5
).

By adapting
[
40
, Subsection 4.2.1]
, it is also possible to endow
([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]},T_{[\Sigma]})
with a Gibbs-Markov structure, which would then yield their own spaces of Lipschitz functions. However, these spaces are much too large, and their Lipschitz norm would depend on
[\Sigma]
. To keep some of our estimates uniform, we do not follow this line. Instead, we adapt the previous results on Gibbs-Markov maps to the specific case where the stopping time
\varphi
comes from an induction process applied to
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
.

Since
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
is recurrent and measure-preserving, the system
([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]},T_{[\Sigma]})
is well-defined and measure-preserving.

Proposition A.8
.

Let
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
be a recurrent Markov
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-extension of a Gibbs-Markov map
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
. Let
\Sigma\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be non-empty and finite. Then:

\displaystyle\rho(\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]})

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\rho_{\ess}(\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]})

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{-1},

where
\Lambda>1
is any expansion constant for
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
.

Proof.

The extension
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
is Markov, so by definition the jump function
F
is constant on elements of the partition
\alpha
. Hence, for all
p\in\Sigma
and
n\geq 1
, we have an almost surely finite stopping time

\varphi_{p,\Sigma,n}(x):=\inf\left\{k\geq 1:\Card\{0<\ell\leq k:S_{\ell}F(x)\in\{q-p:\ q\in\Sigma\}\}=n\right\}.

This stopping time is designed so that, for all
x\in A
,

T_{[\Sigma]}^{n}(x,p)=(T_{\varphi_{p,\Sigma,n}}(x),p+S_{\varphi_{p,\Sigma,n}(x)}F(x)).

For all
p
,
q\in\Sigma
and
n\geq 1
, let

A_{p,q,n}:=\{x\in A:\ (x,p)\in T_{[\Sigma]}^{-n}([q])\},

which is
\sigma(\alpha_{\varphi_{p,\Sigma,n}})
-measurable. Then, for all
p\in\Sigma
and
n\geq 1
, for all
f\in\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}^{n}(f)(\cdot,p)=\sum_{q\in\Sigma}\mathcal{L}_{\varphi_{q,\Sigma,n}}(f(\cdot,q)\mathbf{1}_{A_{q,p,n}}).

By Proposition
A.5
, there exists a constant
C
depending only on the initial Gibbs-Markov map such that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}^{n}(f)(\cdot,p)\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\Sigma}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi_{q,\Sigma,n}}(f(\cdot,q)\mathbf{1}_{A_{q,p,n}})\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\Sigma}C\left[\Lambda^{-\inf_{A_{q,p,n}}\varphi_{q,\Sigma,n}}\int_{A_{q,p,n}}D_{\alpha^{*}}f(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu+\int_{A_{q,p,n}}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\Sigma}C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\mu(A_{q,p,n})\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}}+\int_{A_{q,p,n}}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}}+\sum_{q\in\Sigma}\int_{A_{q,p,n}}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu\right].

Taking the supremum over
p\in\Sigma
yields

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}^{n}(f)\right\|_{\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}}+\sum_{p,q\in\Sigma}\int_{A_{q,p,n}}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\Lambda^{-n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}}+|\Sigma|\int_{[\Sigma]}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu_{[\Sigma]}\right].

The bound on the essential spectral radius
\rho_{\ess}(\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]})
comes from Hennion’s theorem
[
25
]
. The spectral radius is at least
1
since
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}(\mathbf{1})=\mathbf{1}
, and at most
1
because of the Lasota-Yorke inequality and the fact that the spectrum of
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}
oustide
\overline{B}(0,\Lambda^{-1})
is made of eigenvalues of
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}
.
∎

If in addition
([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]},T_{[\Sigma]})
is mixing, then the operator
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma],0}
decays at exponential speed; otherwise, the induced system
([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]},T_{[\Sigma]})
has a period, which we need to take into account. The proofs of the following theorems are classical, albeit lengthy; we refer the reader, for instance, to
[
21
, Corollaire 1.1.18]
and
[
21
, Corollaire 1.1.19]
.

Corollary A.9
.

Let
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
be an ergodic and recurrent Markov
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-extension of a Gibbs-Markov map with data
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
. Assume furthermore that
([0],\mu,T_{[0]})
is mixing.

Then there exist constants
C>0
and
\rho\in(0,1)
such that, for all
f\in\mathcal{B}_{0}
and
n\geq 0
,

\left\|\mathcal{L}_{[0]}^{n}f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq C\rho^{n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}.

(A.4)

Corollary A.10
.

Let
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
be an ergodic and recurrent Markov
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-extension of a Gibbs-Markov map with data
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
. Let
M\geq 1
be such that
\Sp(\mathcal{L}_{[0]}\curvearrowright\mathcal{B})\cap\mathbb{S}_{1}
is the group of
M
th roots of the unit.

Then there exists a partition
A=\bigsqcup_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{/M\mathbb{Z}}}A_{\ell}
such that each
A_{\ell}
is
\sigma(\alpha^{*})
-measurable and
T_{[0]}(A_{\ell})\subset A_{\ell+1}
for all
\ell
. Setting
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}:=\{f\in\mathcal{B}:\ \Supp(f)\subset A_{\ell}\}
,

\mathcal{B}=\bigoplus_{{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{/M\mathbb{Z}}}}\mathcal{B}_{\ell},

and each restriction
\mathcal{L}_{[0]}:\mathcal{B}_{\ell}\to\mathcal{B}_{\ell+1}
is continuous. In addition,
T_{[0]}^{M}:A_{\ell}\to A_{\ell}
is mixing for all
\ell
.

Finally, there exist constants
C>0
and
\rho\in(0,1)
such that, for all
f\in\mathcal{B}
and
n\geq 0
,

\left\|\frac{1}{M}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M-1}\mathcal{L}_{[0]}^{n+\ell}f-\int_{[0]}f\;{\rm d}\mu\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}\leq C\rho^{n}\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}.

(A.5)

We may also adapt Proposition
A.5
to get that
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}
maps a much larger Banach space into
\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}
. As in Section
5
, let
\alpha_{p,\Sigma}
be the partition associated with the stopping time

\varphi_{p,\Sigma}(x):=\inf\{n\geq 1:\ S_{n}F(x)\in\{q-p:\ q\in\Sigma\}\}.

Set
\alpha_{[\Sigma]}:=\{a\times\{p\}:\ a\in\alpha_{p,\Sigma},\ p\in\Sigma\}
. We define a seminorm

|f|_{\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])}:=\sum_{a\times\{p\}\in\alpha_{[\Sigma]}}\mu(a)|f(\cdot,p)|_{\Lip(a)},

and a norm
\left\|f\right\|_{\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])}:=|f|_{\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])}+\left\|f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{1}([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]})}
. Then we get:

Proposition A.11
.

Let
([\mathbb{Z}^{d}],\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{T})
be a recurrent Markov
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-extension of a Gibbs-Markov map
(A,\alpha,d,\mu,T)
. Let
\Sigma\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be non-empty and finite. Then
\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}:\ \Lip^{1}([\Sigma])\to\mathcal{B}_{[\Sigma]}
is continuous.

Proof.

We use the same notation as in the proof of Proposition
A.8
with
n=1
. For all
p
,
q\in\Sigma
,
for all
a\times\{p\}\in\alpha_{[\Sigma]}
, for all
f\in\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])
, by Proposition
A.5
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{L}_{[\Sigma]}(f)(\cdot,p)\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\Sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\times\{q\}\in\alpha_{[\Sigma]}\\
a\subset A_{q,p,1}\end{subarray}}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\varphi_{q,\Sigma,1}}(f(\cdot,q)\mathbf{1}_{a})\right\|_{\mathcal{B}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{q\in\Sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\times\{q\}\in\alpha_{[\Sigma]}\\
a\subset A_{q,p,1}\end{subarray}}C\left[\Lambda^{-1}\mu(a)|f|_{\Lip(a)}+\int_{a}|f|(\cdot,q)\;{\rm d}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=C\Lambda^{-1}|f|_{\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])}+C|\Sigma|\left\|f\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{1}([\Sigma],\mu_{[\Sigma]})}

\displaystyle\leq C|\Sigma|\left\|f\right\|_{\Lip^{1}([\Sigma])}.\qed
